worktree `sweep6/T8`, dev port **4108**.

**Territory (write access):** `public/panels/billdoc.js` · `public/panels/billdoc.d.ts` ·
`public/panels/billcustomer.js` · `docs/NUMBERING.md`

| slice | share | phases | block |
|---|---|---|---|
| reading the code for correctness | 40% | 200 | P03501–P03700 |

Per file: `billdoc.js` 300+ · `billcustomer.js` 55 · `docs/NUMBERING.md` 30 · `billdoc.d.ts` 25.

**Not a finding here** (pre-empted by the prompt, re-checked against `docs/REJECTED-IDEAS.md`):
gaps in the bill/invoice series (deliberate) · the absence of a delete-a-bill permission (R27) ·
the KOT's fixed 280px body overflowing a 58mm roll (R26) · no printer fires on a backup stack.

**Result key:** ✅ pass · ❌ real problem (fixed this run) · ⏭ skipped, with a written reason.
Results below are the **second pass**, re-run from the top after every fix landed.

On a real thermal queue that is the wrong instruction: an 8-line bill declares 80×134mm against 70×65mm media, so the driver scales to 0.49 and rotates, and **the bill printed sideways at half size.** `measure()` is now an intentionally empty function (kept, because the toolbar and the onload path both call it) and paper feed belongs to the queue. **re-run 2026-08-22:** ✅ no page size is injected on either thermal document |

# SWEEP #7 — the 500 NEW checks, `P18601`–`P19100`

Written and executed by terminal 8 of sweep #7, 2026-08-22/23, against `origin/main` **b64951ad**,
branch `sweep7/t8-printing-and-bill`, worktree `../wt-s7-t8`, dev port **4208**.

**Nobody handed me a checklist this time.** These 500 were designed after reading the territory,
against the ground the sweep-#6 block does NOT cover — chiefly:

| block | ids | ground, and why it was chosen |
|---|---|---|
| A | `P18601`–`P18700` | **The clock, swept.** Every date, time, day and financial year on all three documents × 10 boundary instants × 5 time zones × 3 locales. This fault class has now come back FOUR times; sweep #6 checked three of the four surfaces and never the financial year. |
| B | `P18701`–`P18790` | **The money identities the ledger did not cover** — the rebuilt banquet column allocation over 40 line/gap shapes, the round-off's own SIZE (its size is the tell that found F5), and 25 pathological inputs. |
| C | `P18791`–`P18843` | **What the sheet CALLS itself and what it CLAIMS**, over the full gstin × scheme × cancelled × parcel matrix, plus the header-row matrix. |
| D | `P18844`–`P18930` | **The preview's zoom layer.** Landed 2026-08-19 with ZERO ledger rows on it, and it had already broken a row the ledger had passed. |
| E | `P18931`–`P18990` | **The customer sheet driven as a waiter drives it** — the counter, the refusal, the three lookup layers, the lifecycle. |
| F | `P18991`–`P19040` | **Cross-panel truth**: one document, one identity, one number, one word for the tax, everywhere. |
| G | `P19041`–`P19064` | **What the paper LOOKS like** — read as images, then measured on the rendered DOM under print media. |
| H | `P19065`–`P19100` | **Would a real restaurant want it this way.** |

**Result key:** ✅ pass · ❌ real problem (fixed this run) · ⏭ open, with a written reason.
Every row below is a SECOND pass, re-run from the top after the last fix landed.

⚠️ **Two traps this block exists to stop the next sweep falling into.**
1. **A computed style read on the bill LIES on screen.** The preview's fit-zoom scales every
   used length, so `getComputedStyle` returned 2.55px for a 3px border. Emulate **print** media
   before believing any measurement of the paper (it cost two rows this run: P03842, P03812).
2. **`page.setContent()` has no origin, so `localStorage` throws.** Half of block D is untestable
   that way — serve the document from a real http origin via `page.route()` instead.

# ⬛ SWEEP #8 · TERMINAL 8 — THE MANAGER PANEL'S HOST PAGE AND SHELL · `P61701`–`P62700`

**Everything above this line belongs to sweep #6's terminal 8, whose territory was PRINTING, the
bill document and the numbers on it (`P03501`–`P04000`). Nothing above has been touched.** Sweep #8
re-cut the thirty territories from the real file structure, so a ledger file is no longer one
terminal's subject — it is one terminal NUMBER's shelf. This section is sweep #8's terminal 8, and
its subject is a different part of the product entirely.

**Territory (re-derived from the repo, not taken from the prompt):**

```
app/manager/layout.tsx          app/editor/page.tsx        components/PanelFrame.tsx
app/manager/page.tsx            lib/safeAreaBridge.ts      public/panels/editor/index.html
```

Six files, 542 lines. Everything that makes a `100vh` iframe behave on a phone, plus the document
the iframe actually loads. `public/panels/editor/app.js` and `style.css` are **not** in this
territory this round and were only ever READ, to check that the shell agrees with them.

**Dev port 4308.** Branch `sweep8/t8-manager-host-and-shell`, worktree `../wt-s8-t8`.

## What this round did

| | |
|---|---|
| **Pre-existing rows re-run** | **45**, all ✅ — **0 regressions** |
| **New rows written and executed** | **875** of the 1,000-id block (`P61701`–`P62604` used; `P62605`–`P62700` unspent) |
| **Result** | **874 ✅ · 1 ⏭ · 0 ❌** |
| **Problems found and fixed** | **10** — six on the first pass, then items 9, 11, 12 and 13, which the owner picked off the report |
| **Problems found and NOT fixed** | **2** — the unnamed theme toggle (he did not pick it) and the phone geometry of two shared-script controls (he ruled it not needed) |
| **Guard left behind** | `npm run verify:panel-shell` — 37 checks, **32 of 32 sabotages caught** (20 for the first six items, 12 for the owner's four) |

**Every one of the six faults was the same shape:** a line of the shell still described a world the
code around it had moved on from. None was reachable by a type-checker, a unit test or a screenshot
— only by comparing the shell against the code it describes. That is exactly what the new guard
does, and it is why the guard is cross-file rather than a snapshot of the markup.

## The 861 new rows

Planned by ENUMERATING the territory rather than by having ideas: every id the markup declares
(23), every class it uses (36), every script it loads (22), every asset hash (24), every export and
listener in the two host libraries, every one of the six surfaces that embed this shell, and every
rule in `CLAUDE.md` that touches a panel host. Then the checks were written against that list.

## The owner's four, picked off the report and built (2026-09-03)

He read this round's report and answered: *"do 9, 11, 12 and 13 no need for 14"*. All four are in
this branch, each its own commit, and all four crossed a territory line on his word — reported that
way in the chat before they were built, not after.

- **item 9** — the manager panel's LEGACY connection indicator. `connbadge.js`'s pill replaced the
  old text one and this panel was the only one of three that never had the old one removed: the
  pill carried code to HIDE it, and `editor/app.js` went on writing three different words into a
  hidden element in four places. Removed as a whole path — the element, the four writes,
  `connbadge.js`'s hide-branch and `style.css`'s four `.conn` rules — because deleting the markup
  alone would make those four writes throw. Four obituary comments left behind. Driven: the forced
  500 path, which held two of the four writes, still tells the person out loud (`P62587`).
- **item 11** — five admin-console links opened the manager panel through `/editor`, the retired
  address, so every admin panel-open paid an extra redirect and kept a back-compat door
  load-bearing. Also found in passing: the recycle bin listed the SAME screen twice, as "Manager"
  and as "Menu editor". `/editor` itself stays, for links taped up before the rename.
- **item 12** — `:not(.ktext)` and `.dash-card b.ktext` both dead since `dashCard()` stopped
  writing that class. Removed together, and the guard now ties the two to each other: the
  exclusion may exist only while something can apply it, and vice versa.
- **item 13** — `verify:accent-ink` had no row in `docs/GUARD-MAP.md`, which is why
  `verify:pointers` was red. **That red was already on clean `main`** before this round started.

## What is NOT covered by this round, honestly

- **`P62557`** — `#themeToggle` ships as an empty `<button>` with no `title` and no `aria-label`;
  `theme.js` writes both once the DOM is ready, because the wording depends on the saved skin. All
  three panel shells ship the identical empty tag. Reported as item 10; **he did not pick it**.
- **The phone geometry of two injected controls** — the connection pill is 25px tall and the guest
  bell's count is 10.5px at 360px, both below the bars the shell holds for its own controls. Both
  are drawn by shared files. Reported as item 14; **he answered "no need for 14"**. Measured and
  recorded at `P62506`/`P62507` so no later sweep files it again as new.
- **`P62605`–`P62700`** of this block are unspent.

```sh
node scripts/sweep/t8/static2.mjs            # E–F
node scripts/sweep/t8/static3.mjs            # G–I

node scripts/sweep/t8/static5.mjs            # O

npm run verify:panel-shell                   # the guard the six fixes left behind
```

# ⬛ SWEEP #8 · TERMINAL 8 · **ROUND 2** — 500 MORE, ALMOST ALL DRIVEN · `P99301`–`P99800`

**The owner's word, after round 1 was merged and deployed:** *"after making it live and merging plan
500 phases test within your boundaries make sure it cover everthing within your boundries and test
everything again if any error left"*.

**Planned from a MEASUREMENT, not a fresh idea.** Round 1 wrote 875 rows over these six files, and
counting what KIND of evidence they produced is what set this round's shape:

| round 1 produced | rows |
|---|---|
| read the shipped code or config | **731** |
| DROVE the real app and asserted the rendered result | **144** |
| widths driven | **two** (1280 and 360) |
| roles driven | **one**, essentially (manager; one row touched kitchen and tablet) |
| of the SIX surfaces that embed this shell, opened | **one** |

So round 2 is 500 rows aimed at exactly that gap — **487 of them drive the app.** The block
`P99301`–`P99800` was claimed from `INDEX.md` and **pushed to `main` on its own before a single row
was written**, and the highest id actually on disk was re-checked in the same breath (`P98727`).

## What it covers

| section | ids | what it drives |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | `P99301`–`P99360` | who gets in at this door, observed for the manager, the cook, the waiter, the owner, and a visitor whose session ended |
| **B** | `P99361`–`P99420` | an address a person could plausibly arrive with — mistyped, truncated, copied out of a chat, pasted twice, 500 characters long, carrying an emoji — on both manager addresses and the restaurant's own |
| **B2** | `P99421`–`P99432` | the `middleware.ts` added on 2026-09-02 runs on `/r/:path*`, which is also where three STAFF doors live. **This is where round 2's one real fault was.** |
| **C** | `P99433`–`P99500` | all FIVE addresses that embed this shell, opened for real — `/manager`, `/r/<slug>/manager`, the owner console's Manager mode, Menu and Inventory — plus the kitchen and waiter doors, to prove each mounts its OWN shell |
| **D** | `P99501`–`P99550` | the phone in motion: the URL bar sliding, three viewport heights, LANDSCAPE (which round 1 never turned), the keyboard opening, typing, clearing |
| **E** | `P99551`–`P99610` | every one of the shell's ten tabs opened and asserted to render something honest |
| **F** | `P99611`–`P99660` | the drawer opened and closed twenty times, the hardware BACK, the scrim, the hit-test on its ✕, and the keyboard's way round the shell |
| **G** | `P99661`–`P99720` | the five widths round 1 never drove — 320, 390, 768, 1024, 1440 — each measured for overflow, overlap, clipping, tiny text and bar height |
| **H** | `P99721`–`P99760` | the connection pulled: does the shell open, and is the person TOLD in words? |
| **I** | `P99761`–`P99780` | the `?v=` really busts (the served bytes are hashed and compared), and remembered choices survive a reload |
| **J** | `P99781`–`P99800` | five shared scripts blocked one at a time — does the panel still open, keep its chrome, and draw the floor? |

## Result

**500 written · 500 executed · 487 ✅ · 13 ⏭ · 0 ❌.** One real fault, found and fixed.

### The fault

**A member of staff with a damaged bookmark was handed the diner's screen.** The middleware added
on 2026-09-02 turns an address whose percent-escapes are broken into the guest "this menu isn't
available right now — please ask a member of staff" page. Its matcher is `/r/:path*` — and
`/r/<slug>/manager`, `/kitchen`, `/tablet` and `/r/<slug>/login` all live under that same prefix.
Driven on all four: every one landed on `/r/zz-unreadable-address/menu`. A manager whose taped-up
link got cut short was told to ask a member of staff, which is the one sentence that cannot be true
of the person reading it. **The file's own note said it "does not run on the panels"; the matcher
always did.**

Fixed by choosing the dead end from the LAST path segment — a fixed word, never the damaged part:
a diner keeps the guest screen and the QR on their table, a member of staff gets the ordinary staff
sign-in. Re-driven: diner → the guest screen · manager → `/manager` · cook → `/kitchen` · waiter →
`/tablet` · every good address untouched.

### The 13 ⏭, each with its reason on the row

- **7 rows** — the owner console's **Inventory** screen: that module is an ADMIN ENTITLEMENT and it
  is OFF for this restaurant, so the console does not list Inventory at all and `/owner/inventory`
  forwards to `/owner`. The owner is never shown what is withheld (R36). Those rows need a
  restaurant with the module switched on.
- **6 rows** — the **Banquet**, **Inventory** and **Rating review** tabs are not on screen for this
  manager: the first two are admin entitlements that are off here, the third is a manager
  permission this diag user does not hold. The shell's job in all three cases is to render nothing,
  and it does — which is why these are `⏭` and not `❌`.

### Four expectations this round CORRECTED, not faults

Driving found four places where round 1's reading had been right about the substance and wrong
about the shape. Each row says so:

- **A cook or waiter opening `/manager` lands on their OWN panel, not on a password box.** The gate
  does send them to `/login?next=/manager`; the login page then sees their live session and
  forwards them to `ROLE_HOME`. Better than parking a signed-in cook on a form — and the substance
  holds: they never get the manager shell. The rows now assert the frame's SRC, not a frame count.
- **`CLAUDE.md` said there is no `middleware.ts`.** One was added on 2026-09-02, and CLAUDE.md was
  updated in the same change, which is what its own rule asks. So `P99355` no longer asks "does it
  exist" but "does it gate" — and it does not.
- **A label with `text-overflow: ellipsis` is not "clipped".** The restaurant name is capped at
  38vw on purpose and SHOWS that it was shortened; the collapsed desktop rail hides every label on
  purpose and keeps the words in the DOM for a screen reader. The width rows now flag only text cut
  off with no way to know it was.
- **A live socket failing while the plug is out is the plug being out.** It is filtered with the
  other network errors. That message also quotes the whole subscribe URL, which carries a key —
  so `r2lib.mjs` now scrubs keys out of every note before anything is written down.

### One thing measured and deliberately NOT changed

In the collapsed desktop rail the tab COUNT badge is **10px** (`body.nav-rail:not(.nav-rail-open)
.tab-badge`), below the 11px floor the rest of the shell holds to. It is the same class of thing as
the guest bell's 10.5px count, which the owner answered **"no need for 14"** on in round 1's
report. Recorded with its number at `P99670`/`P99682` rather than filed again as new.

## How to re-run round 2

```sh
node scripts/sweep/t8/round2a.mjs --base http://localhost:4308   # A + B  (the door)
node scripts/sweep/t8/round2b.mjs --base http://localhost:4308   # C + D + E
node scripts/sweep/t8/round2c.mjs --base http://localhost:4308   # F – J
npm run verify:panel-shell                                       # 45 checks, 39/39 sabotages caught
```

Every row is a command, not a description of one. Nothing in this round wrote a database row, and
one sign-in per role served the whole run.

---

## ⬛ T8 · THE THIRTEEN ROWS ROUND 2 COULD NOT RUN — RUN PROPERLY (2026-09-03)

**The owner's word: _"do whats left"._** Round 2 skipped 13 rows for one honest reason — Banquet
and Inventory are ADMIN ENTITLEMENTS and both were OFF for this restaurant, and "Rating review" is
a manager permission the diag manager did not hold. So the tabs were correctly not on screen and
`/owner/inventory` forwarded to `/owner` (R36 — the owner is never shown what is withheld).

`scripts/sweep/t8/round2d.mjs` switches the three things on through the admin's OWN screen API
(`POST /api/admin/restaurants/access-tree`), waits out the two server caches (settings ~8s, panel
access 30s) by POLLING rather than sleeping blind, drives the rows, then **puts every switch back
and verifies it is back** — in a `finally` and on `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM`, against values read before
anything was written. This sweep's own scar is a guard that switched a category off across seven
restaurants and then died two steps later.

**Restore point read, and confirmed restored:**
`{"settings":{"banquet_allowed":false,"inventory_allowed":false},"grants":{"view_ratings":false}}`

**Result: 23 rows · 23 ✅ · 0 ❌.** The 13 keep their original ids and are updated in place, above —
never renumbered, because "re-run P99567" has to keep meaning one thing. The 10 below are the
things the switched-on state made checkable for the FIRST time, on ids claimed for exactly that.

| id | check | how to verify | result | note |
|----|-------|---------------|--------|------|
| P99921 | with the module switched on, the admin's own screen agrees it is on | `node scripts/sweep/t8/round2d.mjs` | ✅ |  |
| P99922 | the shell survived rendering three tabs it had never shown before | `node scripts/sweep/t8/round2d.mjs` | ✅ |  |
| P99923 | …and all ten tabs are on screen now, which is the first time that has been true | `node scripts/sweep/t8/round2d.mjs` | ✅ |  |
| P99924 | …and the nav still fits them, or scrolls rather than overflowing silently | `node scripts/sweep/t8/round2d.mjs` | ✅ |  |
| P99925 | …and nothing threw while rendering them | `node scripts/sweep/t8/round2d.mjs` | ✅ |  |
| P99926 | on a phone, the two module tabs appear in the drawer with the rest | `node scripts/sweep/t8/round2d.mjs` | ✅ |  |
| P99927 | …and none of their labels is cut off at 360px | `node scripts/sweep/t8/round2d.mjs` | ✅ |  |
| P99928 | …carrying the inventory-only flag, so the tab IS the whole panel there | `node scripts/sweep/t8/round2d.mjs` | ✅ |  |
| P99929 | …and nothing threw while opening it | `node scripts/sweep/t8/round2d.mjs` | ✅ |  |
| P99930 | …and it does NOT open on the floor — it is the inventory screen | `node scripts/sweep/t8/round2d.mjs` | ✅ |  |

**What it proved, in one line:** with all three switched on, all ten tabs are on screen at once for
the first time in either round, the nav still fits them, both module tabs reach the phone drawer
with their labels intact, and the owner console's Inventory screen mounts THIS shell with the
inventory-only flag, its own skin and its own insets — and every switch went back.
